| head, several shut | N shops are closed — no channel sells at them | N 家门店已关闭 — 所有渠道均不营业 | **COMPOSED** — the same, with the live counter `N 家门店` (`N 家门店中 M 家营业`, the sheet's subtitle) |
| blocked-row explainer | The whole shop is shut, so every channel there is closed and none can be opened on its own. Open X under Shops on the Sales screen first. | 整家门店已关闭，因此该门店的所有渠道均为关闭状态，也无法单独开启。请先在销售页面的「门店」中开启 X。 | **COMPOSED** — live `门店已关闭` + live `所有渠道` + live `页面` + live `销售`, the Sales screen's own nav label, + the section header `门店` quoted in 「」 so it names what is on screen. **Rejected:** `店铺` — not this product's word for a shop anywhere; `门店` is used throughout |
| shop confirm, opening | Every sales channel at this shop opens, and it takes orders, pickups and counter sales again. Meituan follows within about a minute. | 该门店的所有销售渠道将开启，恢复接单、自提与门店收银。美团约一分钟内跟随。 | **COMPOSED** — live `恢复接单` (`public/m.html`, holiday mode) + live `自提` + live `收银` + live `美团约一分钟内` (`02-settings-ops.js` `门店约一分钟内恢复营业`). **Rejected:** stopping at `所有销售渠道将开启` — true and useless: the till and pickup are the half an operator is standing at the counter worrying about |
| shop confirm, closing | Every sales channel at this shop closes, the counter till stops selling, and orders route to the other shop. Orders already placed are not affected. | 该门店的所有销售渠道将关闭，门店收银台停止销售，订单将转由其他门店处理。已下单的订单不受影响。 | **COMPOSED** — live `收银台` + live `已下单的订单不受影响。` taken whole from the per-channel confirm beside it. **Rejected:** omitting the reroute clause — closing a shop moves its delivery and pickup to the other one, and an operator who learns that afterwards learns it from a customer |

**THE LAST SENTENCE OF BOTH CONFIRMS IS THE POINT OF THEM.** This switch writes
every channel at the shop *and* the shop-wide fulfilment flag — it is the
bluntest control in the app, and the screen it now sits on is a phone. Naming
the till, the pickups and the reroute is what stops *close this shop* being read
as *close this shop's website*.

**Nothing is retired** except the footer sentence restructured above.

**Ash should correct any of the five composed pairs if they read wrong to a
Chinese operator; nothing depends on the exact phrasing.**

## When a wallet yuan is counted (2026-09-18, §182)

| where | English | Chinese | provenance |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Finance → Summary, under Revenue | Orders only — wallet money counts when spent, not when topped up. | 仅计订单 —— 储值在消费时计入，充值时不计入。 | **composed** by Claude, from live fragments |
| Reports → Overview, under Revenue | incl. ¥X top-ups | 含充值 ¥X | **lifted whole** from `01-core.js` |

**WHY EITHER SENTENCE EXISTS.** Finance's Revenue row is what customers paid on
orders, recognised at the sale. The Reports card is cash received, which books a
wallet top-up on the day the money lands and values gzgrocery off the stock
ledger. Both are right about their own question and they do not match for the
same window. Before §182 neither screen said which question it was answering,
and the first person to put them side by side reasonably read it as a bug.

**THE FINANCE SENTENCE SPENDS ITS WORDS ON TIMING**, not on what is in the
figure, because the channel lines directly above it already say where the money
came from. What they cannot show is *when* a wallet yuan was counted — and the
timing is the whole of the disagreement with the other screen.

**`储值` RATHER THAN `钱包`, deliberately.** `钱包充值` is live and is the act of
loading the wallet; `储值消费` is live and is the act this row counts — spending
it. The row says "spent" with the character the screen next door already spends
it with, and a reader moving between the two is not asked to learn that two
words are one thing.

**Ash should correct the composed pair if it reads wrong to a Chinese operator;
nothing depends on the exact phrasing.**

## One box of a parked order has left (2026-09-19, §188)

§187 let a parked order send the parcels its shortage does not touch, and
deliberately said nothing to the customer: the order keeps its `awaiting_restock`
status, so `firstDispatch` is never set and the `dispatched` line never fires.
That is right about the ORDER — 订单已发货 is untrue while the kitchen lines have
not been made — and it left the customer with **nothing at all**. A box arriving
unannounced, days before the rest, reads as a short delivery.

**Register: CUSTOMER** (Rule 1) — it is a notification row in the mini app.
**Rule 5 is honoured in the code, not in the words:** `shipsNothing` gates the
send, so a pickup customer and a counter sale never receive this line.

| where | EN | CN | provenance |
|---|---|---|---|
| notification title | Part of your order has shipped | 订单部分已发货 | **COMPOSED** under the standing delegation, 2026-09-19 — the live customer pair `订单已发货` with 部分 inserted |
| notification body | Order {no} — one of the parcels has shipped. The rest follows once the remaining items are back in stock. | 订单 {no} — 其中一个包裹已发货，其余商品待补货后发货。 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19, from live parts only |

**EVERY WORD IN THE BODY IS LIVE; ONLY THE SENTENCE IS NEW.** `订单` and
`已发货` are the customer register's own dispatched line; `包裹` is lifted from
the live operator button `标记此包裹已发货`; `待补货` is the live parcel hold pill
(`待补货 — N 项`); `商品` is the live parcel count (`N 件商品`). One verb
throughout — 发货, never 发出 — because the body says the same thing twice about
two different boxes and two verbs would read as two different events.

Rejected, and why, so the search is not repeated:

- **`分批发货`** — live, and it is exactly this concept, but it is the OPERATOR
  word for the *Ship part later* action on the drawer. Rule 1: a cross-register
  difference is deliberate. The customer is not performing a split; they are
  being told a box has gone.
- **`订单已发货`** alone — live, and the correct customer pair for the whole
  order having shipped. Using it here would tell the customer the order is on
  its way while the fresh half has not been made, which is the one thing §187
  refused to say. That refusal is the reason this section exists.
- **`部分发货`** — reads as an instruction or a state label rather than news;
  `订单部分已发货` keeps the live sentence's shape (订单 … 已发货) so the two
  notifications look like siblings in the list, which they are.

**NO PUSH, AND THAT IS A DECISION ABOUT THE CUSTOMER'S CONSENT, NOT ABOUT THE
WORDS.** A WeChat subscribe message costs one one-shot consent per send, and the
send worth spending it on is the one saying the whole order is on its way. So
these strings appear in the in-app notification list only; `ORDER_STATUS_NOTIF`
is untouched, which is also what stops the push firing for a status the order
does not hold.

**FLAGGED, NOT BUILT:** the parcel's MODE is not named — the customer is told
*one of the parcels*, not *the 陆运 parcel*. Naming it would be more useful and
every mode word is already a locked pair (`陆运`, `空运`), but the notification
body is a single sentence in two languages and the mode belongs to the parcel,
not the order. Worth doing if Ash wants it; not invented here.

## A box that went out on an order that was cancelled (2026-09-19, §189)

§187 stopped a cancel crediting the shelf with units sitting in a courier's van.
That made the count honest and told nobody: the money went back, the number was
right, and a box on its way to a person who is no longer a customer had no screen
anywhere saying so. This is the telling, and the answer to it.

**Register: OPERATOR** (Rule 1) — the shipment card in the order drawer, the
orders chip, and the Needs-you row on Home. Nothing here reaches a customer:
they have already been refunded and told the order was cancelled.

**ALMOST ALL OF IT IS COPIED.** 收回 is a live pair and it is exactly this verb
about exactly this kind of object — something the shop handed out, being taken
back — so the only composing is the two words built on it.

| where | EN | CN | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| card heading | This parcel was already out when the order was cancelled | 订单取消时此包裹已发货 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — from live `已取消`, live `此包裹已发货` (the parcel dispatch button) |
| card sub | Say what became of it. Only "it came back" puts the stock on the shelf. | 请确认其去向。仅“已收回”会把库存退回货架。 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — `退回` is the live waste-void wording for stock going back |
| button / closed pill | It came back / Recovered | 已收回 | live: `Taken back · 已收回`, `public/retail-admin/05-marketing.js` |
| button / closed pill | It did not come back / Not recovered | 未收回 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — the live `收回` negated |
| orders chip | To recover | 待收回 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — live `收回` with the live `待` of `待补货` / `待分配` |
| Needs-you row | Parcels to recover | 待收回包裹 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — the chip plus live `包裹` |
| confirm (recovered) | Put this parcel back on the shelf? | 把此包裹退回货架? | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 |
| confirm (not recovered) | Record this parcel as not recovered? | 登记此包裹未收回? | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — `登记` is the live waste verb for writing a movement down |

**WHY 收回 AND NOT 退回.** Both are live. `退回` is what STOCK does — the waste
void "puts the stock back", 库存退回 — and it is used here for exactly that, in
the confirm body and nowhere else. `收回` is what the SHOP does to a thing it
handed out, which is the coupon take-back's own sense and is the action being
asked about. One verb for the goods moving, one for the shop retrieving them;
collapsing the two would make "did it come back" and "does the shelf get it"
read as the same question, and the whole point of this control is that they are
not.

Rejected, and why:

- **`退回中`** — live, and tempting: it is the tracking status *Returning to
  sender* on this very screen. It names a parcel the CARRIER is already
  returning, which is one way a box comes back and not the only one, and it is a
  state the system observes rather than a question it is asking. A control
  labelled with an observed state would read as a claim about where the box is.
- **`召回`** — recall, in the product-safety sense. Wrong register entirely and
  frightening on an order screen.
- **`作废`** — live (waste void), but it means voiding a RECORD, not retrieving
  goods.

**THE CHIP IS REGISTERED IN THE ADMIN DICTIONARY**
(`public/retail-admin/10-extras.js`), which its neighbour is not: `Needs a call`
has had no entry since §98 and has been showing an English chip in a Chinese
admin ever since. Recorded here because it is a live gap somebody should close,
and because not repeating it was a decision rather than an accident.

## Which box has gone (2026-09-19, §190)

§188 told the customer of a parked order that *one of the parcels* had shipped.
On a split order that is two boxes arriving on different days, so the sentence
made them count boxes to work out which one had landed. Ash: *"ok do it"*, on
the flag §188 left.

**Register: CUSTOMER** (Rule 1). **Nothing here is a new word** — every mode
already has a locked pair, and `Air · 空运` / `Land · 陆运` are already
customer-facing on the printed slip (`lib/feie.js`, whose own comment says the
receipt is customer-facing). Only the three sentences are composed, and each is
a live mode word in front of §188's live `包裹已发货`.

| mode | EN | CN | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `air` | the air parcel has shipped | 空运包裹已发货 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — live `空运` + §188's live `包裹已发货` |
| `land` | the land parcel has shipped | 陆运包裹已发货 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — live `陆运` + the same |
| `local` | the same-city parcel has shipped | 同城包裹已发货 | **COMPOSED**, 2026-09-19 — live `同城` (`public/m.html` parcel word) + the same |
| *anything else* | one of the parcels has shipped | 其中一个包裹已发货 | §188, unchanged |

**THE FALLBACK IS NOT DEAD CODE AND IS NOT A GUESS.** A supplier parcel cannot
reach this sentence — the dispatch route refuses those outright — so the branch
exists for a mode added LATER, whose customer word will not exist on the day it
is added. A new mode should read slightly vague rather than wrong, and a
fallback is what stops the next person inventing a pair in a hurry to fill it.

**同城 RATHER THAN 同城配送.** The longer form is live too, in the operator
drawer's mode pill (`Local delivery · 同城配送`), and it is the OPERATOR
register's word for the shipping method. The customer is being told about a box,
not about a method, and `public/m.html` already shortens it to `同城` for exactly
that reading. Rule 1: the cross-register difference is deliberate.

**THE TITLE IS UNCHANGED** — `Part of your order has shipped · 订单部分已发货`.
The mode belongs in the body beside the order number, where the customer is
already reading a specific fact about a specific parcel; a title that varied by
mode would give one notification three shapes in the list.


## A parcel the shortage is holding (2026-09-19, §193)

§187 gave the parked order's healthy box its dispatch button back and refused
the box that carries a short line by name — `parcel_awaiting_restock`. The code
went to the UI and **no pair was registered for it**, so `tdErrorText` fell
through to its last resort and printed *Parcel awaiting restock* in English, in
a Chinese admin, on both routes that raise it. §193 makes the JD waybill route a
third caller and the busiest one, which is what turned a quiet gap into a
sentence somebody reads.

**Register: OPERATOR** (Rule 1). **Nothing here is composed.** Both halves are
live verbatim and the pair is their join.

| EN | CN | source |
|---|---|---|
| That parcel is waiting on a restock. | 该包裹正在等待补货。 | **LIFTED**, 2026-09-19 — `该包裹` from `invalid_shipment_id` in the same dictionary (`该包裹不属于此订单。`); `正在等待补货。` verbatim from `lib/push-events.js` (`顾客已付款，或订单正在等待补货。`) |

**THE SUBJECT IS THE BOX, NOT THE LINE INSIDE IT.** What the server actually
knows is that this parcel holds one or more products the order is parked on —
`parcelHeldLines` counts them. A sentence naming the count was considered and
dropped: the operator has the hold pill on the same screen for that
(`待补货 — N 项`, live since §187), the toast is the refusal and not the report,
and an error string that has to be built from a number is one more place for the
two to disagree about one parcel.

Rejected, and why:

- **`该订单正在等待补货。`** — the order, not the parcel. Live-adjacent and
  wrong in the one way this whole section exists to prevent: §187's rule is that
  a parked ORDER is not a parked BOX, and an error saying *the order* is waiting
  would tell the operator the opposite of what the button beside it does.
- **`该包裹尚不可发货。`** — lifted from `not_shippable`'s live
  `该订单尚不可发货。` by swapping the noun. Readable, and it names the refusal
  without naming the cause, which is the exact complaint that produced §193: the
  operator's next question is *why*, and `待补货` is the word every other
  control on that screen already answers it with.
- **`缺货`** — live in the product-stock sense (*out of stock*). It describes
  the shelf, not this order's wait, and the admin already reserves `待补货` /
  `等待补货` for the wait.

## A pre-order's restock window — the checkout popup (2026-09-20)

Rule 4 is worked in order under the standing delegation of *The delegation
becomes standing*: a live pair first, the source second, composing last. **Every
word below is live**; what is composed is one clause and the join.

### The customer register — what a shopper reads at checkout

| key | EN | CN | provenance |
|---|---|---|---|
| `preorderWindow` (range) | `2-4 days` | `2-4 天` | numeral + the live day counter `天`; nothing composed |
| `preorderWindow` (single) | `3 days` / `1 day` | `3 天` | as above; only the EN plural branches |
| `preorderRestockClause` | `they are usually back in stock within {window}` | `预计 {window} 内补货` | **COMPOSED** by Claude (Opus), 2026-09-20 — all three words live |

**THE SENTENCE AROUND IT IS NOT COMPOSED AND IS NOT RETIRED.** The default
message a tenant sees with no window set is the one that has shipped since this
notice existed, character for character:

> 您订单中的部分商品目前缺货。此为预订订单，我们将在补货后尽快为您一并发货。

With a window set, the clause is inserted and nothing else moves:

> 您订单中的部分商品目前缺货。此为预订订单，**预计 2-4 天内补货**，我们将在补货后尽快为您一并发货。

**HOW THE CLAUSE WAS ARRIVED AT, word by word.** `预计` is live and
customer-facing in two places for exactly this sense — an estimate, not a
recorded fact: the order page's `预计送达` and the fresh-batch notice's
`预计 {delivery_date} 新鲜送达`. That matters under Rule 2, which bans a word that
asserts a past event from labelling an estimate; `预计` is the product's own
estimate word and carries the hedge the English "usually" carries. `补货` is live
in the very sentence this clause joins (`我们将在补货后尽快为您一并发货`) and in the
order status `等待补货`, so the clause and its neighbours name the same event with
the same word. `内` is live as the within-a-span particle in `48 小时内` (the
fresh-batch tip) and `30 天内` (the sign-in screen). `天` is the live day counter
throughout.

**TWO CANDIDATES WERE CONSIDERED AND REJECTED, recorded so the search is not
repeated.** **`到货`** — live, and the more natural word for goods ARRIVING —
names arrival at the customer's door, which is not what this window measures: the
estimate is when OUR shelf is refilled, and the courier leg is still ahead of it.
Using it would promise a delivery date the system has not computed, which is the
§138 exposure this notice exists inside. **`通常`** ("usually") maps the English
hedge more literally than `预计` does, but it is not live anywhere in this product
and `预计` already carries the hedge in the register a customer meets it in, so
the live pair wins under preference 1.

**REGISTER: CUSTOMER** (Rule 1). The orders-list chip and the order drawer keep
`预订` whatever a tenant writes here, and that is deliberate — see the operator
table below.

### The operator register — the back-office card

Every pair here is admin-facing. Where the Fresh batches card already had a pair
for the same control it is **lifted whole**, so an operator who has configured
one card meets no new vocabulary on the other.

| key | EN | CN | provenance |
|---|---|---|---|
| `preorderCard` | `Pre-orders` | `预订` | LIFTED — the notice's own live kicker |
| `preorderPopup` | `Checkout popup` | `结账弹窗` | LIFTED from the Fresh batches card |
| `preorderEyebrow` | `Popup eyebrow` | `弹窗小标题` | LIFTED from the Fresh batches card |
| `preorderTitle` | `Popup title` | `弹窗标题` | LIFTED from the Fresh batches card |
| `preorderNotice` | `Checkout notice` | `结账提示` | LIFTED from the Fresh batches card |
| `preorderWindowField` | `Restock window` | `预计补货时间` | **COMPOSED** by Claude (Opus), 2026-09-20 — `预计` + `补货` + `时间`, all live |
| `preorderWindowFrom` | `From` | `最快(天)` | **COMPOSED** by Claude (Opus), 2026-09-20 |
| `preorderWindowTo` | `To` | `最慢(天)` | **COMPOSED** by Claude (Opus), 2026-09-20 |
| `preorderHubDefault` | `Default wording · no time named` | `默认文案 · 未提时间` | **COMPOSED** by Claude (Opus), 2026-09-20 — `默认文案` lifted from the Fresh batches card's *any field blank* line |
| `preorderHubCustom` | `Own wording` | `自定义文案` | **COMPOSED** by Claude (Opus), 2026-09-20 |
| `preorderHubWindow` | `Back in stock within X` | `预计 X 内补货` | the customer clause above, reused on the hub status line |

**`最快` / `最慢` RATHER THAN `最少` / `最多`.** The boxes hold days of waiting,
and fast/slow is how a wait is described; least/most reads as a quantity of goods,
which is what every other numeric box on these screens holds. Neither pair is
live for this control. **Ash should correct either if it reads wrong to a Chinese
operator; nothing depends on the exact phrasing.**

**THE OPERATOR CHIP DOES NOT FOLLOW THE TENANT'S COPY, and that is Rule 1 rather
than a gap.** A tenant who renames the popup's eyebrow to something seasonal does
not rename the `预订` chip on the orders list or in the order drawer. Cross-register
differences are deliberate: an operator scanning a list needs one stable word for
the state whatever sentence the storefront is currently running.

**`{window}` IS THE ONLY PLACEHOLDER, AND IT CAN BE ABSENT, WHICH NO OTHER
PLACEHOLDER IN THIS PRODUCT CAN.** `{ship_date}`, `{delivery_date}` and
`{make_date}` are computed when their notice fires and always resolve. This one
is typed and can be cleared, so operator copy that asks for a window with no days
behind it falls back to the built-in sentence whole. A half-sentence
(`…内补货` with nothing before it, or the English `within .`) must never reach a
customer, and the failure would be invisible to whoever cleared the boxes.

## One word for a reservable row — the browse band (2026-09-20)

**RETIREMENT, not a composition.** Nothing is minted here. One live pair stops
being drawn in one register and another live pair takes its place there.

| key | EN | CN | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bandReservable` | PRE-ORDER | 可预订 | **LIVE, now on every browse surface** |
| `bandReservable` (PDP buy button) | PRE-ORDER | 预订 | LIVE, unchanged — a button says what it does |
| ~~`bandShipsSoon`~~ | ~~SHIPS SOON~~ | ~~现订现发~~ | **RETIRED FROM THE BAND** — still live as the popup title |

**WHAT WAS WRONG.** The shop grid and the web storefront card drew
`SHIPS SOON · 现订现发`. The PDP badge, the PDP buy button, the PDP size rows,
the shop's variant size sheet and the favourites tile drew `PRE-ORDER · 可预订`.
Same product, same shelf state, two words — and the word changed as the shopper
tapped from the card into the page. Rule 1 calls a within-register difference a
defect, and this was one, five surfaces to two.

**HOW THE SPLIT SURVIVED.** It was written down as a reason rather than as a
defect: the grid's own note argued the website *"names the promise instead of
the mechanism"*. That is a real distinction and it is not being called wrong —
it is being outvoted by the five surfaces that never adopted it. It also fought
the window §195 appends: **`SHIPS SOON · 2-4 DAYS` says soon and then says how
long**, which is the reading the band exists to save a shopper from.

`scripts/test_fresh_today_label.js` had been asserting both halves in a single
case whose NAME read *"a GROCERY row is the carve-out: no batch is being cooked,
so PRE-ORDER stands"* while its second line asserted `SHIPS SOON`. The test knew.

**WHERE THE RETIRED PAIR STAYS, AND WHY THAT IS NOT A HALF-RETIREMENT.**
`现订现发` remains the default TITLE of the checkout pre-order popup
(`computePreorderNotice`). Rule 1's two registers: the band names the STATE a
shopper is browsing, the popup titles the PROMISE at the moment they commit.
Retiring it there was never the change, and a tenant may now rewrite it anyway
(§194).

**`预订` VS `可预订` ON THE PDP BUTTON IS DELIBERATE AND UNCHANGED.** The badge
says what the row IS (`可预订` — reservable); the button says what the tap DOES
(`预订` — reserve). A button that reads "reservable" describes itself instead of
offering an action. Both are live and both predate this entry.

### The window in the band's register

| key | EN | CN | provenance |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bandRestockWindow` (range) | `2-4 DAYS` | `2-4 天` | numerals + the live `天`; upper case because the band is |
| `bandRestockWindow` (single) | `3 DAYS` / `1 DAY` | `3 天` | as above; only the EN plural branches |

Drawn as `PRE-ORDER · 2-4 DAYS` / `可预订 · 2-4 天`, on the separator
`withFreshArrival` already uses — a reader comparing two rows must not be able
to tell which appender drew which.

**THE SAME NUMBER APPEARS IN TWO CASES AND THAT IS A REGISTER DIFFERENCE, NOT
DRIFT.** The band is upper case (`2-4 DAYS`); the checkout popup says the same
window lower case inside a sentence (`usually back in stock within 2-4 days`).
Chinese has no case, so both read `2-4 天` and the two must be identical there —
`scripts/test_preorder_band.js` asserts exactly that, and folds the English case
out before comparing the four implementations.

**A ZERO-DAY WINDOW IS NOT DRAWN, IN ANY REGISTER.** `0/0` would read
`PRE-ORDER · 0 DAYS` on the band and *"within 0 days"* in the popup. The first
is §80's defect — a label describing a wait that is not happening — and the
second is not a sentence anyone writes on purpose. Treated as unset everywhere.
A range that merely STARTS at zero (`0-2 天`) is a real window and is kept.

**NOT DRAWN ON THE ADD-TO-ORDER PICKER**, which takes the renamed word and
neither appender. §170 ruled that an add-on picker is not a browse surface and
kept its date off that screen; a window arriving without that date would leave
the list reading `PRE-ORDER · 2-4 DAYS` beside a bare `NEXT BATCH`. Both
appenders arrive together or neither does.
